# MANIFEST – knowledge-base/

Every file in this folder, its size and its SHA-256, plus the counts and the exact list of ways these files differ from the project
repository. `README.md` explains what the corpus is; this file is the audit trail. `MANIFEST.md` cannot list itself.

Totals: **3,879 files** (3,878 listed below plus this manifest), **61,962,993 bytes** without this manifest.

## Built from

`scripts/build_knowledge_base.py`, which refuses to run unless the four pinned workbooks still match `corpus_snapshot.json`
(SHA-256 `a7524e8f2c3c07d3fd6253a2995d3307374177bb72ad6a1e5bfa05dc1355660d`), and unless the corpus, the source texts and the pin all name the same 1,290 documents.

| Pinned workbook | Rows | SHA-256 |
|---|---:|---|
| `cjp_columns_curated_normalized.xlsx` | 803 | `bf56b42a920c5af3809efcef3a1f60606ceb7ec0f0a273e100dfc890d3b442ec` |
| `cjp_books_curated_normalized.xlsx` | 303 | `379913ac7b5d175d7b57b241e7238969cf78afedb7dba24672bee739b77adee8` |
| `cjp_speeches_curated_normalized.xlsx` | 154 | `58ef24c202187a4099235863b32c02281b7f8adec07aa7dd546db7f80ab705b2` |
| `cjp_biography_curated_normalized.xlsx` | 35 | `3303ab6bfac55a73fc0f2bd9472289ac1f4b3cbbbf342f4b354ffc83ab485d04` |

## Counts

| Area | Columns | Books | Speeches | Biography | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `data/text/<doc_id>.md` | 803 | 299 | 153 | 35 | 1,290 |
| `data/enriched/*.csv` rows | 803 | 299 | 153 | 35 | 1,290 |
| `corpus/**/<doc_id>.md` cards | 803 | 299 | 153 | 35 | 1,290 |
| `corpus/**/<doc_id>.json` cards | 803 | 299 | 153 | 35 | 1,290 |

Also: `corpus/topic_map.json`, `corpus/voice_card.md`, `corpus/router_prompt.md`, `README.md` and this file.

**The arithmetic.** The four pinned workbooks hold 1,295 rows (803 + 303 + 154 + 35). Five documents are retired, so
1,295 − 5 = **1,290** documents are live. `data/text/` holds one file per live document, so it holds 1,290 files. The
retired documents have no file in `data/text/` to remove, so the five are not subtracted a second time.

## Differences from the project repository

The delivered files are byte-for-byte the repository files except for the three changes below. Nothing else was altered.

1. **Line endings.** Every file here uses LF. 153 of the 1,290 source texts (153 book chapters) use CRLF in the repository, where they are exact copies of their sources; in this folder they are LF. `voice_card.md` and `router_prompt.md` were also CRLF in the repository (2 files) and are LF here.
2. **An internal processing tag removed from 168 source texts (168 book chapters).** At the end of the `Source:` header line these files carried a parenthesised note recording which build stage split the chapter out. It is a project-internal note, not part of the source; it is removed so old and new documents are indistinguishable. Nothing else in those files changes.
3. **One cross-reference rewritten.** A curated note in speech `SC090` (field `register_markers`) named the retired duplicate of speech `SB085`. In `data/enriched/speeches.csv` and in `corpus/speeches/C_biographical_personal/SC090.json` it now names `SB085`, the surviving copy, as the retired-documents register records. This is the only descriptive text in the folder that differs from the pinned workbook (two places: the CSV cell and its card).

## Search indexes (not in this folder)

The binary indexes stay in the project repository under `data/index/`. They are large, rebuilt by scripts, and not part of this delivery. Sizes are exact.
The build date is taken from the index’s own metadata file where it records one and from the file’s modified date where it does not.

| File | Bytes | Built | What it is for |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `data/index/corpus_dense.npy` | 41,622,656 | 2026-09-26 | Meaning vectors: one 768-number vector for each of the 13,549 passages (model BAAI/bge-base-en-v1.5). Drives search by meaning. |
| `data/index/pilot_dense.npy` | 41,622,656 | 2026-09-26 | An exact copy of corpus_dense.npy under the file name the running app loads. |
| `data/index/pilot_sparse.pkl` | 19,642,430 | 2026-09-26 | Keyword index (BM25 over the curated phrases and the passage text). Drives exact-phrase search. |
| `data/index/corpus_mean.npy` | 3,200 | 2026-09-27 | The average passage vector. It is subtracted from every vector before two are compared; all three routing thresholds assume this 'centred' scale. |
| `data/index/topic_centroids.npy` | 92,288 | 2026-09-27 | Thirty topic vectors, one per dimension: the centred average of that dimension's passages. Used to place a question or a document in a dimension. |

Companion lookup tables (JSON), also kept in the repository:

| File | Bytes | Built | What it is for |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `data/index/sparse_phrase_dict.json` | 536,343 | 2026-09-26 | Phrase dictionary behind the keyword index. |
| `data/index/date_index.json` | 152,990 | 2026-09-27 | One entry per document: its date and how precise it is (day, month or year). Books and biography chapters dated 1 January are labelled year. |
| `corpus/index/chunk_index.json` | 312,632 | 2026-09-27 | Per-document lookup into the passages, with the pin the passages were cut from. |
| `corpus/index/chunks.jsonl` | 21,407,211 | 2026-09-27 | The 13,549 passages themselves (every document cut into 200-400 token pieces). |

## Topic filing

Route sources across the 1,290 cards: matcher 1,045, affinity 227, orphan 18 (sum 1,290).

Documents with no topic (`primary` is `null`): `BA052`, `BA099`, `CA071`, `CA234`, `CA471`, `CA520`, `CA521`, `CC025`, `CC026`, `CC082`, `CC092`, `CE009`, `CE075`, `GC015`, `GC018`, `SA123`, `SB065`, `SE092`.

Documents filed by a keyword matcher although their affinity to any dimension is below the 0.31 floor (22): `BA012`, `BA026`, `BA101`, `BE004`, `CA055`, `CA196`, `CA201`, `CA208`, `CA220`, `CA283`, `CA287`, `CA334`, `CA441`, `CA497`, `CC095`, `CE004`, `CE016`, `CE020`, `CE053`, `CE077`, `GC012`, `SD143`.

**Known limitation – topic routing.** Topic routing is newly rebuilt and has not been validated end to end. The routing measured here is the closest-dimension router in the app’s retrieval code, which compares a question with the centre of each of the 30 dimensions. On 30 short questions, one per dimension, it named the intended dimension first for 26 of 30 (86.7%) and within its top three for 26 of 30 (86.7%). Four dimensions never won their own question: `judiciary_milestones_and_tributes` (“Tell me about the centenary.” went to `foundation_for_liberty_and_prosperity`); `public_funds_budget_and_bank_evidence` (“What is the pork barrel?” went to `economy_taxes_and_prosperity`, with a closeness of 0.0938, under the router’s 0.12 out-of-scope line); `twin_beacons_doctrine` (“What is the twin-beacons doctrine?” went to `international_law_disputes`); `ill_gotten_wealth_and_the_pcgg` (“What is ill-gotten wealth?” went to `economy_taxes_and_prosperity`). One of the four is the project’s flagship concept, the twin-beacons doctrine. A second set of 60 longer, descriptive questions (two per dimension) did better – 86.7% first and 100% within three, with every dimension winning at least one of its own questions – but 8 questions still went to a wrong first choice. Chance for 30 dimensions is 3.3% first and 10% within three, so the router is doing real work; it is not yet reliable enough to trust unattended. Two things were not measured: the live robot’s own router (a language-model call driven by router_prompt.md, which needs an API key that was not available) and end-to-end answer quality. On the frozen 40-query retrieval set, 14 of 40 selected result sets changed after the move to the 30-dimension map and gold hit@10 fell by 2.9 points, while hit@1, hit@3 and hit@5 did not move. Routing was deliberately not adjusted in this delivery. CE-11 to CE-14 are the gate before the robot is demonstrated.
